Oh, I'm sorry, I hope I didn't explain that poorly just now. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Um, well... I'd say they happen pretty consistently. It's not just a one-time thing, unfortunately. I'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds awful.

Oh... um, well, I guess if I really think about it... yes. I hope it's okay to admit that. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or that I can't handle my responsibilities, but... I suppose this feeling has actually been building for a while now. 

I'd say it's been going on for about two years and four months? It all started right around the time we had a major company expansion, and they put me in charge of the new arts-and-commerce division. 

At first, I just told myself it was the normal pressure of taking on a much bigger role, you know? I wanted to do a perfect job and prove they made the right choice, so I tried to just push through it. But that underlying feeling of things being... off... it just sort of lingered in the background, long before the sounds ever started. 

I'm so sorry, is that the kind of timeline you were looking for? I really hope I'm not giving you too much useless background information about my job. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions exactly how you need me to.

It happens through the conference room walls at the office, or sometimes coming from my cell phone, even when it’s completely turned off. It’s usually my staff or the board members, and they're saying that I’m ruining the company. It just sounds so incredibly clear that I actually answer them out loud... and then I realize no one else around me heard it. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terrible. Am I explaining that alright? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices talking about you, are there ever any actual people around or any devices that could be making the sounds, or do they happen even when there’s no possible source nearby?\n\nPatient: Oh, I'm sorry, I hope I didn't explain that poorly just now. I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. \n\nUm, well, like I mentioned a second ago, my cell phone will be completely powered down—like, totally off, black screen, just sitting there on my desk—and I still hear them coming right out of it. And with the conference room... I'll actually walk over and open the door to check, and it's completely empty. There's no one in there at all. \n\nSo, I guess... no, there isn't anyone actually around, and the devices aren't on. It happens when there's no possible source there. I feel so silly admitting that out loud, and I really hope you don't think less of me for it. I'm just trying so hard to keep it together at the office. \n\nIs... is that the kind of detail you were looking for? I hope I'm answering this the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that others don’t seem to share, like believing something is happening or being directed at you that others say isn’t real?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think I have anything like that. I mean, obviously I have the... the hearing things, which I know is already a lot, and I'm really sorry if that makes me a difficult case for you. \n\nBut as far as beliefs or thoughts that other people think aren't real... no, I don't think so. I'm still very grounded in my day-to-day work, so my thoughts are mostly just about, you know, normal business operations, quarterly reports, and trying to keep the board happy. I try to stay very rational about everything else. \n\nI really hope I'm not misunderstanding your question. Is it okay that I don't have that? I just want to make sure I'm being as helpful as possible for your assessment. Am I giving you what you need?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something is happening to you that others don’t seem to notice or agree with—like something is being hidden from you, or that you have a special ability or role that others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, definitely not. Nothing like that. I'm sorry if I'm repeating myself, I just want to make sure I'm being clear. \n\nI don't think anyone is hiding anything from me, and I certainly don't have any kind of... special abilities or anything grand like that. I'm just a normal person trying to run a business, you know? I rely on my team, and I think we all communicate pretty openly, for the most part. I don't feel like I'm part of some secret plot or that I have some hidden destiny. \n\nI know the voices I mentioned before probably make it sound like I'm completely out of touch with reality, and I'm so sorry if that's the impression I'm giving. But I promise, I don't have any strange beliefs like that. I just want to get back to being a good leader for my company. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too defensive, I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear these voices, and have they been happening consistently over a period of time—like most days—or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd say they happen pretty consistently. It's not just a one-time thing, unfortunately. I'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds awful. \n\nThe... the voices coming from the walls or, you know, when my phone is off... it's been going on for about the past three months now. Almost every day. I really tried to ignore it at first, hoping it would just go away so I wouldn't have to bother anyone with it, but it's just been very persistent for those three months. \n\nI hope that's not too long of a time to have let this go on without getting help. I really should have come in sooner, shouldn't I? I just didn't want to make a fuss. Is three months the kind of timeframe you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you the details you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, felt, or experienced anything else that others around you don’t seem to notice—like seeing something that isn’t there or feeling something touching you when no one is?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, definitely not. I haven't seen anything that isn't there, or... or felt anything touching me. I'm really glad to say I don't have any of those issues. \n\nIt's really just the... the sounds I mentioned earlier. I know that's already more than enough, and I'm so sorry to be taking up your time with it, but my vision and everything else is completely normal. I'm staring at screens and reading over contracts all day for work, so I feel like I'd definitely notice if my eyes were playing tricks on me. \n\nI hope that's a good sign? Like, maybe it means my case isn't quite as bad as it could be? I just really want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to evaluate me properly. Am I answering these okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed your thoughts becoming jumbled or hard to follow, or do you find yourself speaking in a way that others have trouble understanding?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I really hope I haven't been sounding jumbled to you today! I'm so sorry if I've been rambling or if I'm hard to follow right now. \n\nBecause of my job, I have to lead meetings and give directions to my staff all day, so I try to be very careful and precise with my words. Nobody on my team has ever mentioned having trouble understanding me, and I feel like my thoughts are usually pretty organized. I really pride myself on being a clear communicator. \n\nI know the other things I told you about are... well, they're probably a lot to take in, but my thinking itself feels perfectly normal. I really try my best to stay sharp. Am I making sense to you right now? I just want to make sure I'm giving you clear answers and not making your job harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others have told you isn’t true—even if you heard it from them or read it somewhere—and you just couldn’t shake the feeling that it was real?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, I really don't think I've ever experienced that. I always try to be very open to feedback, especially with my role at the company. If my team or the board tells me I've got something wrong, I'm usually the first to admit it and adjust course. I'd hate to be one of those stubborn people who just refuses to listen to reason, you know? \n\nSo, no, I don't hold onto things if people show me they aren't true. I'm really trying to be as objective and rational as possible about everything. I really hope I'm not frustrating you by saying no to so many of these questions! I just want to assure you that aside from the... the sounds I mentioned, I'm staying very grounded in reality. Is this the kind of detail you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least a month where you felt like something was consistently wrong or off, even before these voices started happening?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well, I guess if I really think about it... yes. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. I... I really don't think so. I mean, everyone has a stressful afternoon here and there, especially when you're managing a whole company, but I definitely haven't been down like that for weeks at a time. 

I always make a point to keep a positive attitude. My team really relies on me to set the tone, so I can't afford to just lose interest in things. I wouldn't want anyone to think I wasn't fully capable or that I was letting my responsibilities slide. I really try to stay upbeat and focused.

I hope it isn't frustrating for you that I keep saying no to these things. I promise I'm really searching my memory, I just... I want to make sure I'm being a good patient and giving you exactly what you need. Am I doing okay with these answers?

I'd hate to be one of those stubborn people who just refuses to listen to reason, you know? \n\nSo, no, I don't hold onto things if people show me they aren't true. I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least a month where you felt like something was consistently wrong or off, even before these voices started happening?\n\nPatient: Oh... um, well, I guess if I really think about it... yes. It all started right around the time we had a major company expansion, and they put me in charge of the new arts-and-commerce division. \n\nAt first, I just told myself it was the normal pressure of taking on a much bigger role, you know?
